of God</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Attributes</th><td class="infobox-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409"/><div class="plainlist"><ul><li>Papal vestments</li><li><a href="/wiki/Papal_tiara" title="Papal tiara">Papal tiara</a></li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below"><a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="Pope Pius (disambiguation)">Other popes  named Pius</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Pope Pius VII</b> (<a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Pio VII</i>; born <b>Barnaba Niccolò Maria Luigi Chiaramonti</b>;<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> 14 August 1742 – 20 August 1823), was head of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> and ruler of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> from 14 March 1800 to his death in August 1823. Chiaramonti was also a <a href="/wiki/Monk" title="Monk">monk</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Saint_Benedict" class="mw-redirect" title="Order of Saint Benedict">Order of Saint Benedict</a> in addition to being a well-known <a href="/wiki/Theologian" class="mw-redirect" title="Theologian">theologian</a> and bishop.
</p><p>Chiaramonti was made <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Tivoli" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Tivoli">Bishop of Tivoli</a> in 1782, and resigned that position upon his appointment as <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Imola" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Imola">Bishop of Imola</a> in 1785. That same year, he was made a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">cardinal</a>. In 1789, the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolution" title="French Revolution">French Revolution</a> took place, and as a result a series of anti-clerical governments came into power in the country. In 1798,<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> during the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolutionary_Wars" title="French Revolutionary Wars">French Revolutionary Wars</a>, French troops under <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_Bonaparte" class="mw-redirect" title="Napoleon Bonaparte">Napoleon Bonaparte</a> invaded Rome and captured <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_VI" title="Pope Pius VI">Pope Pius VI</a>, taking him as a prisoner to France, where he died in 1799. The following year, after a <i><a href="/wiki/Sede_vacante" title="Sede vacante">sede vacante</a></i> period lasting approximately six months, Chiaramonti was elected to the papacy, taking the name Pius VII.
</p><p>Pius at first attempted to take a cautious approach in dealing with Napoleon. With him he signed the <a href="/wiki/Concordat_of_1801" title="Concordat of 1801">Concordat of 1801</a>, through which he succeeded in guaranteeing religious freedom for Catholics living in France, and was present at his coronation as <a href="/wiki/Emperor_of_the_French" title="Emperor of the French">Emperor of the French</a> in 1804. In 1809, however, during the <a href="/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars" title="Napoleonic Wars">Napoleonic Wars</a>, Napoleon once again invaded the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a>, resulting in his excommunication through the papal bull <i><a href="/wiki/Quum_memoranda" title="Quum memoranda">Quum memoranda</a></i>. Pius VII was taken prisoner and transported to France. He remained there until 1814 when, after the French were defeated, he was permitted to return to Rome, where he was greeted warmly as a hero and defender of the faith.
</p><p>Pius lived the remainder of his life in relative peace. His papacy saw a significant growth of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_the_United_States" title="Catholic Church in the United States">Catholic Church in the United States</a>, where Pius established several new dioceses. Pius VII died in 1823 at age 81.
</p><p>In 2007, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> began the process towards <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonizing</a> him as a saint, and he was granted the title <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Biography">Biography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Biography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Pius_VII_by_Antonio_Canova_1805,_Albertinum,_Dresden.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Pope_Pius_VII_by_Antonio_Canova_1805%2C_Albertinum%2C_Dresden.jpg/170px-Pope_Pius_VII_by_Antonio_Canova_1805%2C_Albertinum%2C_Dresden.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Pope_Pius_VII_by_Antonio_Canova_1805%2C_Albertinum%2C_Dresden.jpg/255px-Pope_Pius_VII_by_Antonio_Canova_1805%2C_Albertinum%2C_Dresden.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Pope_Pius_VII_by_Antonio_Canova_1805%2C_Albertinum%2C_Dresden.jpg/340px-Pope_Pius_VII_by_Antonio_Canova_1805%2C_Albertinum%2C_Dresden.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2056" data-file-height="3088" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Pius_VII_by_Antonio_Canova_1805,_Albertinum,_Dresden.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pope Pius VII by <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Canova" title="Antonio Canova">Antonio Canova</a> 1805, <a href="/wiki/Albertinum" title="Albertinum">Albertinum</a>, Dresden</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:142px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cesena,_casa_natale_di_papa_pio_VII_chiaramonti_01.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Cesena%2C_casa_natale_di_papa_pio_VII_chiaramonti_01.JPG/140px-Cesena%2C_casa_natale_di_papa_pio_VII_chiaramonti_01.JPG" decoding="async" width="140" height="187" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Cesena%2C_casa_natale_di_papa_pio_VII_chiaramonti_01.JPG/210px-Cesena%2C_casa_natale_di_papa_pio_VII_chiaramonti_01.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Cesena%2C_casa_natale_di_papa_pio_VII_chiaramonti_01.JPG/280px-Cesena%2C_casa_natale_di_papa_pio_VII_chiaramonti_01.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2736" data-file-height="3648" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cesena,_casa_natale_di_papa_pio_VII_chiaramonti_01.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The birthplace of Pius VII</div></div></div>
<p>Barnaba Niccolò Maria Luigi Chiaramonti was born in <a href="/wiki/Cesena" title="Cesena">Cesena</a> in 1742, the youngest son of <a href="/wiki/Count" title="Count">Count</a> Scipione Chiaramonti (30 April 1698 – 13 September 1750). His mother, Giovanna Coronata (d. 22 November 1777), was the daughter of the <a href="/wiki/Marquess" title="Marquess">Marquess</a> Ghini; through her, the future Pope Pius VII was related to the <a href="/wiki/Braschi_family" title="Braschi family">Braschi</a> family of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_VI" title="Pope Pius VI">Pope Pius VI</a> after marriage on 10 November 1713.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="E. E. Y. Hales&#39; book The Emperor and the Pope claims that Chiaramonti was not related to the Braschi family, though his contemporaries thought so. (January 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Though his family was of noble status, they were not wealthy but rather, were of middle-class stock.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His maternal grandparents were Barnaba Eufrasio Ghini and Isabella de' conti Aguselli. His paternal grandparents were Giacinto Chiaramonti (1673–1725) and Ottavia Maria Altini; his paternal great-grandparents were Scipione Chiaramonti (1642–1677) and Ottavia Maria Aldini. His paternal great-great-grandparents were Chiaramonte Chiaramonti and Polissena Marescalchi.
</p><p>His siblings were Giacinto Ignazio (19 September 1731 – 7 June 1805), Tommaso (19 December 1732 – 8 December 1799) and Ottavia (1 June 1738 – 7 May 1814).
</p><p>Like his brothers, he attended the Collegio dei Nobili in <a href="/wiki/Ravenna" title="Ravenna">Ravenna</a> but decided to join the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Saint_Benedict" class="mw-redirect" title="Order of Saint Benedict">Order of Saint Benedict</a> at the age of 14 on 2 October 1756 as a novice at the <a href="/wiki/Abbey_of_Santa_Maria_del_Monte" class="mw-redirect" title="Abbey of Santa Maria del Monte">Abbey of Santa Maria del Monte</a> in Cesena.  Two years after this on 20 August 1758, he became a professed member and assumed the name of <i>Gregorio</i>. He taught at Benedictine colleges in <a href="/wiki/Parma" title="Parma">Parma</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, and was <a href="/wiki/Holy_orders_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy orders (Catholic Church)">ordained</a> a <a href="/wiki/Priesthood_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Priesthood (Catholic Church)">priest</a> on 21 September 1765.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Episcopate_and_cardinalate">Episcopate and cardinalate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Episcopate and cardinalate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>A series of promotions resulted after his relative, Giovanni Angelo Braschi, was elected <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_VI" title="Pope Pius VI">Pope Pius VI</a> (1775–99). A few years before this election occurred, in 1773, Chiaramonti became the personal confessor to Braschi. In 1776, Pius VI appointed the 34-year-old <a href="/wiki/Dom_(title)" class="mw-redirect" title="Dom (title)">Dom</a> Gregory, who had been teaching at the <a href="/wiki/Monastery" title="Monastery">Monastery</a> of <a href="/wiki/Sant%27Anselmo" class="mw-redirect" title="Sant&#39;Anselmo">Sant'Anselmo</a> in Rome, as honorary <a href="/wiki/Abbot" title="Abbot">abbot</a> <i><a href="/wiki/In_commendam" title="In commendam">in commendam</a></i> of his monastery. Although this was an ancient practice, it drew complaints from the monks of the community, as monastic communities generally felt it was not in keeping with the <a href="/wiki/Rule_of_St._Benedict" class="mw-redirect" title="Rule of St. Benedict">Rule of St. Benedict</a>.
</p><p>In December 1782, the pope appointed Dom Gregory as the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Tivoli" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Tivoli">Bishop of Tivoli</a>, near <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>. Pius VI soon named him, in February 1785, the <a href="/wiki/San_Callisto" title="San Callisto">Cardinal-Priest of San Callisto</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> and as the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Imola" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Imola">Bishop of Imola</a>, an office he held until 1816.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolutionary_Army" title="French Revolutionary Army">French Revolutionary Army</a> <a href="/wiki/Italian_campaigns_of_the_French_Revolutionary_Wars" title="Italian campaigns of the French Revolutionary Wars">invaded Italy</a> in 1797, <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">Cardinal</a> Chiaramonti counseled temperance and submission to the newly created <a href="/wiki/Cisalpine_Republic" title="Cisalpine Republic">Cisalpine Republic</a>. In a letter that he addressed to the people of his diocese, Chiaramonti asked them to comply "... in the current circumstances of change of government (...)" to the authority of the victorious general Commander-in-Chief of the French army. In his <a href="/wiki/Christmas" title="Christmas">Christmas</a> <a href="/wiki/Homily" title="Homily">homily</a> that year, he asserted that there was no opposition between a <a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">democratic</a> form of government and being a good Catholic: "Christian virtue makes men good democrats.... Equality is not an idea of philosophers but of Christ...and do not believe that the Catholic religion is against democracy."<sup id="cite_ref-bokenkotter_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bokenkotter-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Papacy">Papacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Papacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope Pius VII</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:C_o_a_Pius_VII.svg" class="image"><img alt="C o a Pius VII.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/C_o_a_Pius_VII.svg/60px-C_o_a_Pius_VII.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="70" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/C_o_a_Pius_VII.svg/90px-C_o_a_Pius_VII.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/C_o_a_Pius_VII.svg/120px-C_o_a_Pius_VII.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1006" data-file-height="1179" /></a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Holiness</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Election">Election</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Election">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_1799%E2%80%931800" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, 1799–1800">Papal conclave, 1799–1800</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:167px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ile_san_giorgio.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Ile_san_giorgio.jpg/165px-Ile_san_giorgio.jpg" decoding="async" width="165" height="123" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Ile_san_giorgio.jpg/248px-Ile_san_giorgio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Ile_san_giorgio.jpg/330px-Ile_san_giorgio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="448" data-file-height="335" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ile_san_giorgio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Site of the papal conclave that elected Pius VII</div></div></div>
<p>Following the death of Pope Pius VI, by then virtually France's prisoner, at <a href="/wiki/Valence,_Dr%C3%B4me" title="Valence, Drôme">Valence</a> in 1799, the <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave" title="Papal conclave">conclave</a> to elect his successor met on 30 November 1799 in the Benedictine <a href="/wiki/San_Giorgio_Monastery" title="San Giorgio Monastery">Monastery of San Giorgio</a> in Venice. There were three main candidates, two of whom proved to be unacceptable to the <a href="/wiki/Habsburgs" class="mw-redirect" title="Habsburgs">Habsburgs</a>, whose candidate, <a href="/wiki/Alessandro_Mattei" title="Alessandro Mattei">Alessandro Mattei</a>, could not secure sufficient votes. However, <a href="/wiki/Carlo_Bellisomi" title="Carlo Bellisomi">Carlo Bellisomi</a> also was a candidate, though not favoured by Austrian cardinals; a "virtual veto"<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup> was imposed against him in the name of <a href="/wiki/Franz_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Franz II">Franz II</a> and carried out by Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Franziskus_Herzan_von_Harras" title="Franziskus Herzan von Harras">Franziskus Herzan von Harras</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After several months of stalemate, <a href="/wiki/Jean-Sifrein_Maury" title="Jean-Sifrein Maury">Jean-Sifrein Maury</a> proposed Chiaramonti as a compromise candidate. On 14 March 1800, Chiaramonti was elected pope, certainly not the choice of die-hard opponents of the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolution" title="French Revolution">French Revolution</a>, and took as his pontifical name Pius VII in honour of his immediate predecessor.<sup id="cite_ref-bokenkotter_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bokenkotter-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> He was <a href="/wiki/Papal_coronation" title="Papal coronation">crowned</a> on 21 March, in the adjacent <a href="/wiki/San_Giorgio_Maggiore_(church),_Venice" title="San Giorgio Maggiore (church), Venice">monastery church</a>, by means of a rather unusual ceremony, wearing a <a href="/wiki/Papier-m%C3%A2ch%C3%A9" title="Papier-mâché">papier-mâché</a> <a href="/wiki/Papal_Tiara#Design" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal Tiara">papal tiara</a>.  The French had seized the tiaras held by the Holy See when occupying Rome and forcing Pius VI into exile. The new pope then left for Rome, sailing on a barely seaworthy <a href="/wiki/Austria" title="Austria">Austrian</a> ship, the <i>Bellona</i>, which lacked even a <a href="/wiki/Galley_(kitchen)" title="Galley (kitchen)">galley</a>. The twelve-day voyage ended at <a href="/wiki/Pesaro" title="Pesaro">Pesaro</a> and he proceeded to Rome.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Negotiations_and_exile">Negotiations and exile</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Negotiations and exile">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Jacques-Louis_David_018.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Jacques-Louis_David_018.jpg/220px-Jacques-Louis_David_018.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="263" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Jacques-Louis_David_018.jpg/330px-Jacques-Louis_David_018.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Jacques-Louis_David_018.jpg/440px-Jacques-Louis_David_018.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3622" data-file-height="4338" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Jacques-Louis_David_018.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i><a href="/wiki/Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_VII" title="Portrait of Pope Pius VII">Portrait of Pope Pius VII</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Oil_on_wood" class="mw-redirect" title="Oil on wood">oil on wood</a> by <a href="/wiki/Jacques-Louis_David" title="Jacques-Louis David">Jacques-Louis David</a> (1805, <a href="/wiki/Louvre" title="Louvre">Louvre</a>, <a href="/wiki/Paris" title="Paris">Paris</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>One of Pius VII's first acts was appointing the <a href="/wiki/Minor_orders" title="Minor orders">minor cleric</a> <a href="/wiki/Ercole_Consalvi" title="Ercole Consalvi">Ercole Consalvi</a>, who had performed so ably as secretary to the recent conclave, to the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a> and to the office of <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a>. Consalvi immediately left for France, where he was able to negotiate the <a href="/wiki/Concordat_of_1801" title="Concordat of 1801">Concordat of 1801</a> with the <a href="/wiki/First_Consul" class="mw-redirect" title="First Consul">First Consul</a> <a href="/wiki/Napoleon" title="Napoleon">Napoleon</a>. While not effecting a return to the old Christian order, the treaty did provide certain civil guarantees to the Church, acknowledging "the Catholic, Apostolic, and Roman religion" as that of the "majority of French citizens".<sup id="cite_ref-georgetown1_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-georgetown1-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The main terms of the concordat between France and the pope included:
</p>
<ul><li>A proclamation that "Catholicism was the religion of the great majority of the French" but was not the official religion, maintaining religious freedom, in particular with respect to <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestants</a>.</li>
<li>The Pope had the right to depose <a href="/wiki/Bishop" title="Bishop">bishops</a>.</li>
<li>The state would pay clerical salaries and the clergy swore an oath of allegiance to the state.</li>
<li>The church gave up all claims to church lands that were taken after 1790.</li>
<li>Sunday was reestablished as a "festival", effective <a href="/wiki/Easter" title="Easter">Easter</a> Sunday, 18 April 1802.</li></ul>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pie_VII_Arrestation_par_le_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Radet.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Pie_VII_Arrestation_par_le_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Radet.png/220px-Pie_VII_Arrestation_par_le_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Radet.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="234" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Pie_VII_Arrestation_par_le_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Radet.png/330px-Pie_VII_Arrestation_par_le_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Radet.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Pie_VII_Arrestation_par_le_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Radet.png/440px-Pie_VII_Arrestation_par_le_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Radet.png 2x" data-file-width="2280" data-file-height="2430" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pie_VII_Arrestation_par_le_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Radet.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The arrest of Pius VII</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Le_pape_pie_VII_recevant_l%27extr%C3%AAme_onction.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Le_pape_pie_VII_recevant_l%27extr%C3%AAme_onction.jpg/220px-Le_pape_pie_VII_recevant_l%27extr%C3%AAme_onction.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Le_pape_pie_VII_recevant_l%27extr%C3%AAme_onction.jpg/330px-Le_pape_pie_VII_recevant_l%27extr%C3%AAme_onction.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a8/Le_pape_pie_VII_recevant_l%27extr%C3%AAme_onction.jpg 2x" data-file-width="440" data-file-height="286" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Le_pape_pie_VII_recevant_l%27extr%C3%AAme_onction.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pius VII receives <a href="/wiki/Extreme_unction" class="mw-redirect" title="Extreme unction">extreme unction</a> while Napoleon's prisoner in 1812</div></div></div>
<p>As pope, he followed a policy of cooperation with the French-established Republic and Empire. He was present at the coronation of Napoleon I in 1804. He even participated in France's <a href="/wiki/Continental_Blockade" class="mw-redirect" title="Continental Blockade">Continental Blockade</a> of <a href="/wiki/Great_Britain" title="Great Britain">Great Britain</a>, over the objections of his Secretary of State Consalvi, who was forced to resign. Despite this, France occupied and <a href="/wiki/Annexation" title="Annexation">annexed</a> the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> in 1809 and took Pius VII as their prisoner, exiling him to <a href="/wiki/Savona" title="Savona">Savona</a>. On 15 November 1809 Pius VII consecrated the church at La Voglina, Valenza Po, Piemonte with the intention of the villa La Voglina becoming his spiritual base whilst in exile. Unfortunately his residency was short lived once Napoleon became aware of his intentions of establishing a permanent base and he was soon exiled to France. Despite this, the pope continued to refer to Napoleon as "my dear son" but added that he was "a somewhat stubborn son, but a son still".
</p><p>This exile ended only when Pius VII signed the Concordat of Fontainebleau in 1813. One result of this new treaty was the release of the exiled cardinals, including Consalvi, who, upon re-joining the papal retinue, persuaded Pius VII to revoke the concessions he had made in it. This Pius VII began to do in March 1814, which led the French authorities to re-arrest many of the opposing prelates. Their confinement, however, lasted only a matter of weeks, as Napoleon <a href="/wiki/Abdication" title="Abdication">abdicated</a> on 11 April of that year.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> As soon as Pius VII returned to Rome, he immediately revived the <a href="/wiki/Inquisition" title="Inquisition">Inquisition</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Index_Librorum_Prohibitorum" title="Index Librorum Prohibitorum">Index of Condemned Books</a>.
</p><p><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cardinal_Bartolomeo_Pacca&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Cardinal Bartolomeo Pacca (page does not exist)">Cardinal Bartolomeo Pacca</a>, who was kidnapped along with Pope Pius VII, took the office of Pro-Secretary of State in 1808 and maintained his memoirs during his exile.  His memoirs, written originally in Italian, have been translated into English (two volumes)<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> and describe the ups and down of their exile and the triumphant return to Rome in 1814.
</p><p>Pius VII's imprisonment did in fact come with one bright side for him. It gave him an aura that recognized him as a living martyr, so that when he arrived back in Rome in May 1814, he was greeted most warmly by the Italians as a hero.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relationship_with_Napoleon_I">Relationship with Napoleon I</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Relationship with Napoleon I">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Jacques-Louis_David_-_The_Coronation_of_Napoleon_(1805-1807).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Jacques-Louis_David_-_The_Coronation_of_Napoleon_%281805-1807%29.jpg/220px-Jacques-Louis_David_-_The_Coronation_of_Napoleon_%281805-1807%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="138" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Jacques-Louis_David_-_The_Coronation_of_Napoleon_%281805-1807%29.jpg/330px-Jacques-Louis_David_-_The_Coronation_of_Napoleon_%281805-1807%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Jacques-Louis_David_-_The_Coronation_of_Napoleon_%281805-1807%29.jpg/440px-Jacques-Louis_David_-_The_Coronation_of_Napoleon_%281805-1807%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6000" data-file-height="3773" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Jacques-Louis_David_-_The_Coronation_of_Napoleon_(1805-1807).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pope Pius VII presided over the <a href="/wiki/Coronation_of_Napoleon_I" class="mw-redirect" title="Coronation of Napoleon I">coronation of Napoleon I</a>, as depicted by <a href="/wiki/Jacques-Louis_David" title="Jacques-Louis David">Jacques-Louis David</a></div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_and_the_Catholic_Church" title="Napoleon and the Catholic Church">Napoleon and the Catholic Church</a></div>
<p>From the time of his election as pope to the fall of <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_I_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Napoleon I of France">Napoleon</a> in 1815, Pius VII's reign was completely taken up in dealing with France.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
He and the Emperor were continually in conflict, often involving the French military leader's wishes for concessions to his demands. Pius VII wanted his own release from exile as well as the return of the Papal States, and, later on, the release of the 13 "Black Cardinals", i.e., the cardinals, including Consalvi, who had snubbed the marriage of Napoleon to <a href="/wiki/Marie_Louise,_Duchess_of_Parma" title="Marie Louise, Duchess of Parma">Marie Louise</a>, believing that his previous marriage was still valid, and had been exiled and impoverished in consequence of their stand,<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> along with several exiled or imprisoned prelates, priests, monks, nuns and other various supporters.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Restoration_of_the_Jesuits">Restoration of the Jesuits</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Restoration of the Jesuits">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Suppression_of_the_Society_of_Jesus" title="Suppression of the Society of Jesus">Suppression of the Society of Jesus</a></div>
<p>On 7 March 1801, Pius VII issued the brief "Catholicae fidei" that approved the existence of the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">Society of Jesus</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a> and appointed its first superior general as Franciszek Kareu. This was the first step in the restoration of the order. On 31 July 1814, he signed the papal bull <i>Sollicitudo omnium ecclesiarum</i> which universally restored the Society of Jesus. He appointed <a href="/wiki/Tadeusz_Brzozowski" title="Tadeusz Brzozowski">Tadeusz Brzozowski</a> as the Superior General of the order.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:102px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Pius_VII_statue.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Pope_Pius_VII_statue.JPG/100px-Pope_Pius_VII_statue.JPG" decoding="async" width="100" height="133" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Pope_Pius_VII_statue.JPG/150px-Pope_Pius_VII_statue.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Pope_Pius_VII_statue.JPG/200px-Pope_Pius_VII_statue.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2736" data-file-height="3648" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Pius_VII_statue.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Vatican_Museums" title="Vatican Museums">Vatican Museums</a></div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Opposition_to_slavery">Opposition to slavery</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Opposition to slavery">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Pius VII joined the declaration of the 1815 <a href="/wiki/Congress_of_Vienna" title="Congress of Vienna">Congress of Vienna</a>, represented by Cardinal Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/Ercole_Consalvi" title="Ercole Consalvi">Ercole Consalvi</a>, and urged the suppression of the slave trade. This pertained particularly to places such as <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a> and <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a> where slavery was economically very important. The pope wrote a letter to <a href="/wiki/King_Louis_XVIII" class="mw-redirect" title="King Louis XVIII">King Louis XVIII</a> of France dated 20 September 1814 and to the King <a href="/wiki/John_VI_of_Portugal" title="John VI of Portugal">John VI of Portugal</a> in 1823 to urge the end of slavery. He condemned the slave trade and defined the sale of people as an injustice to the dignity of the human person. In his letter to the King of Portugal, he wrote: "the pope regrets that this trade in blacks, that he believed having ceased, is still exercised in some regions and even more cruel way. He begs and begs the King of Portugal that it implement all its authority and wisdom to extirpate this unholy and abominable shame."
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Reinstitution_of_Jewish_Ghetto">Reinstitution of Jewish Ghetto</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Reinstitution of Jewish Ghetto">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Under Napoleonic rule, the Jewish <a href="/wiki/Roman_Ghetto" title="Roman Ghetto">Roman Ghetto</a> had been abolished and Jews were free to live and move where they would. Following the restoration of Papal rule, Pius VII re-instituted the confinement of Jews to the Ghetto, having the doors closed at nighttime.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_activities">Other activities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Other activities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">August 2018</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Pius VII issued an encyclical "Diu satis" in order to advocate a return to the values of the <a href="/wiki/Gospel" title="Gospel">Gospel</a> and universalized the feast of <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Sorrows" title="Our Lady of Sorrows">Our Lady of Sorrows</a> for 15 September. He condemned <a href="/wiki/Freemasonry" title="Freemasonry">Freemasonry</a> and the movement of the <a href="/wiki/Carbonari" title="Carbonari">Carbonari</a> in the encyclical <a href="/wiki/Ecclesiam_a_Jesu_Christo" title="Ecclesiam a Jesu Christo">Ecclesiam a Jesu Christo</a> in 1821. Pius VII asserted that Freemasons must be excommunicated and it linked them with the Carbonari, an anti-clerical revolutionary group in Italy. All members of the Carbonari were also excommunicated.
</p><p>Pius VII was multilingual and had the ability to speak Italian, French, English and <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:197px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pie_VII_Travaux_pour_l%27ob%C3%A9lisque.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Pie_VII_Travaux_pour_l%27ob%C3%A9lisque.png/195px-Pie_VII_Travaux_pour_l%27ob%C3%A9lisque.png" decoding="async" width="195" height="209" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Pie_VII_Travaux_pour_l%27ob%C3%A9lisque.png/293px-Pie_VII_Travaux_pour_l%27ob%C3%A9lisque.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Pie_VII_Travaux_pour_l%27ob%C3%A9lisque.png/390px-Pie_VII_Travaux_pour_l%27ob%C3%A9lisque.png 2x" data-file-width="1959" data-file-height="2097" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pie_VII_Travaux_pour_l%27ob%C3%A9lisque.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pius VII reviews plans for the obelisk on the <a href="/wiki/Quirinal_Hill" title="Quirinal Hill">Quirinal Hill</a>.</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cultural_innovations">Cultural innovations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Cultural innovations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Pius VII was a man of culture and attempted to reinvigorate Rome with archaeological excavations in Ostia which revealed ruins and icons from ancient times. He also had walls and other buildings rebuilt and restored the <a href="/wiki/Arch_of_Titus" title="Arch of Titus">Arch of Titus</a>. He ordered the construction of fountains and piazzas and erected the obelisk at <a href="/wiki/Monte_Pincio" class="mw-redirect" title="Monte Pincio">Monte Pincio</a>.
</p><p>The pope also made sure Rome was a place for artists and the leading artists of the time like <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Canova" title="Antonio Canova">Antonio Canova</a> and <a href="/wiki/Peter_von_Cornelius" title="Peter von Cornelius">Peter von Cornelius</a>. He also enriched the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Library" title="Vatican Library">Vatican Library</a> with numerous manuscripts and books. It was Pius VII who adopted the yellow and white flag of the Holy See as a response to the Napoleonic invasion of 1808.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonizations_and_beatifications">Canonizations and beatifications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Canonizations and beatifications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Throughout his pontificate, Pius VII canonized a total of five saints. On 24 May 1807, Pius VII canonized <a href="/wiki/Angela_Merici" title="Angela Merici">Angela Merici</a>, <a href="/wiki/Benedict_the_Moor" title="Benedict the Moor">Benedict the Moor</a>, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Colette" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Colette">Colette Boylet</a>, <a href="/wiki/Francis_Caracciolo" title="Francis Caracciolo">Francis Caracciolo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hyacintha_Mariscotti" title="Hyacintha Mariscotti">Hyacintha Mariscotti</a>. He beatified a total of 27 individuals including <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Oriol" title="Joseph Oriol">Joseph Oriol</a>, <a href="/wiki/Berardo_dei_Marsi" title="Berardo dei Marsi">Berardo dei Marsi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Maria_Tomasi" title="Giuseppe Maria Tomasi">Giuseppe Maria Tomasi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Crispin_of_Viterbo" title="Crispin of Viterbo">Crispin of Viterbo</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Consistories">Consistories</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Consistories">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Pius_VII" title="Cardinals created by Pius VII">Cardinals created by Pius VII</a></div>
<p>Pius VII created 99 cardinals in nineteen consistories including notable ecclesial figures of that time such as Ercole Consalvi, <a href="/wiki/Bartolomeo_Pacca" title="Bartolomeo Pacca">Bartolomeo Pacca</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Carlo_Odescalchi" title="Carlo Odescalchi">Carlo Odescalchi</a>. The pope also named his two immediate successors as cardinals: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XII" title="Pope Leo XII">Annibale della Genga</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_VIII" title="Pope Pius VIII">Francesco Saverio Castiglioni</a> (the latter of whom it is said Pius VII and his successor would refer to as "Pius VIII").
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:162px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Leviation_of_pope_Pius_VII_while_Reading_Mass_Wellcome_M0005442.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Leviation_of_pope_Pius_VII_while_Reading_Mass_Wellcome_M0005442.jpg/160px-Leviation_of_pope_Pius_VII_while_Reading_Mass_Wellcome_M0005442.jpg" decoding="async" width="160" height="221" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Leviation_of_pope_Pius_VII_while_Reading_Mass_Wellcome_M0005442.jpg/240px-Leviation_of_pope_Pius_VII_while_Reading_Mass_Wellcome_M0005442.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Leviation_of_pope_Pius_VII_while_Reading_Mass_Wellcome_M0005442.jpg/320px-Leviation_of_pope_Pius_VII_while_Reading_Mass_Wellcome_M0005442.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2793" data-file-height="3856" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Leviation_of_pope_Pius_VII_while_Reading_Mass_Wellcome_M0005442.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The "miracle" of Pius VII in 1811.</div></div></div>
<p>In addition, Pius VII named 12 cardinals whom he reserved "<i><a href="/wiki/In_pectore" title="In pectore">in pectore</a></i>". One died before his nomination could ever be published (he was originally nominated in the 1804 consistory), Marino Carafa di Belvedere resigned his cardinalate on 24 August 1807 upon citing a lack of family descendance, and <a href="/wiki/Carlo_Odescalchi" title="Carlo Odescalchi">Carlo Odescalchi</a> resigned the cardinalate on 21 November 1838 to enter the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">Society of Jesus</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-SMC_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SMC-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1801, according to Remigius Ritzler, Pius VII nominated Paolo Luigi Silva as a cardinal <i>in pectore</i>, however, he died before his name could be published. As a result, Pius VII added the Archbishop of Palermo <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Pignatelli_di_Belmonte" title="Domenico Pignatelli di Belmonte">Domenico Pignatelli di Belmonte</a> in his place. In the March 1816 consistory, the former <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Diocese_of_Saint-Malo" title="Ancient Diocese of Saint-Malo">bishop of Saint-Malo</a> <a href="/wiki/Gabriel_Cortois_de_Pressigny" title="Gabriel Cortois de Pressigny">Gabriel Cortois de Pressigny</a> was among the cardinals created <i>in pectore</i> in the consistory, though he declined the promotion. Similarly, Giovanni Alliata declined the pope's offer for elevation in the same consistory. According to Niccolò del Re, the <a href="/wiki/Vice_Camerlengo_of_the_Holy_Roman_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Vice Camerlengo of the Holy Roman Church">Vice-Camerlengo</a> Tiberio Pacca would have been created a cardinal either in the March 1823 consistory, or a future one held by the pope. However, he suggests that a series of controversies beginning in 1820 prevented the pope from naming him to the Sacred College.<sup id="cite_ref-SMC_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SMC-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_possible_miracle_of_Pius_VII">The possible miracle of Pius VII</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: The possible miracle of Pius VII">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 15 August 1811 - the <a href="/wiki/Feast_of_the_Assumption" class="mw-redirect" title="Feast of the Assumption">Feast of the Assumption</a> - it is recorded that the pope celebrated Mass and was said to have entered a trance and began to levitate in a manner that drew him to the altar. This particular episode aroused great wonder and awe among attendants which included the French soldiers guarding him who were in disbelief of what had occurred.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relationship_with_the_United_States">Relationship with the United States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Relationship with the United States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>' undertaking of the <a href="/wiki/First_Barbary_War" title="First Barbary War">First Barbary War</a> to suppress the Muslim <a href="/wiki/Barbary_pirates" title="Barbary pirates">Barbary pirates</a> along the southern <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_Sea" title="Mediterranean Sea">Mediterranean</a> coast, ending their <a href="/wiki/Barbary_slave_trade" title="Barbary slave trade">kidnapping of Europeans</a> for ransom and <a href="/wiki/Slavery" title="Slavery">slavery</a>, Pius VII declared that the United States "had done more for the cause of Christianity than the most powerful nations of Christendom have done for ages."<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For the United States, he established several new dioceses in 1808 for <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Boston" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Boston">Boston</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_New_York" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of New York">New York City</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Philadelphia" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Philadelphia">Philadelphia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Bardstown" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Bardstown">Bardstown</a>. In 1821, he also established the dioceses of <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Charleston" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Charleston">Charleston</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Richmond" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Richmond">Richmond</a> and <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Cincinnati" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Cincinnati">Cincinnati</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Condemnation_of_heresy">Condemnation of heresy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Condemnation of heresy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 3 June 1816, Pius VII condemned the works of <a href="/wiki/Melkite_Greek_Catholic_Church" title="Melkite Greek Catholic Church">Melkite</a> bishop <a href="/wiki/Germanos_Adam" title="Germanos Adam">Germanos Adam</a>. Adam's writings supported <a href="/wiki/Conciliarism" title="Conciliarism">conciliarism</a>, the view that the authority of <a href="/wiki/Ecumenical_council" title="Ecumenical council">ecumenical councils</a> was greater than that of the papacy.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Death_and_burial">Death and burial</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Death and burial">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1822, Pius VII reached his 80th birthday and his health was visibly declining. On 6 July 1823, he <a href="/wiki/Hip_fracture" title="Hip fracture">fractured his hip</a> in a fall in the papal apartments and was bedridden from that point onward. In his final weeks he would often lose consciousness and would mutter the names of the cities that he had been ferried away to by the French forces. With the <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a> <a href="/wiki/Ercole_Consalvi" title="Ercole Consalvi">Ercole Consalvi</a> at his side, Pius VII died on 20 August at 5 a.m.
</p><p>He was briefly interred in the <a href="/wiki/List_of_extant_papal_tombs" title="List of extant papal tombs">Vatican grottoes</a> but was later buried in a monument in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a> after his funeral on 25 August.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:177px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Grab_Pius_VII.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Grab_Pius_VII.jpg/175px-Grab_Pius_VII.jpg" decoding="async" width="175" height="274" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Grab_Pius_VII.jpg/263px-Grab_Pius_VII.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Grab_Pius_VII.jpg/350px-Grab_Pius_VII.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3492" data-file-height="5474" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Grab_Pius_VII.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The tomb of Pius VII in Saint Peter's Basilica.</div></div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatification_process">Beatification process</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Beatification process">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above n" style="background-color:gold;"><div class="honorific-prefix" style="display:inline;font-size: 77%; font-weight: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a></div><br /><div style="display:inline;" class="fn">Pius VII</div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:Teodoro_Matteini,_Ritratto_di_Pio_VII,_1801.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Teodoro Matteini, Ritratto di Pio VII, 1801.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Teodoro_Matteini%2C_Ritratto_di_Pio_VII%2C_1801.jpg/220px-Teodoro_Matteini%2C_Ritratto_di_Pio_VII%2C_1801.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="287" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Teodoro_Matteini%2C_Ritratto_di_Pio_VII%2C_1801.jpg/330px-Teodoro_Matteini%2C_Ritratto_di_Pio_VII%2C_1801.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Teodoro_Matteini%2C_Ritratto_di_Pio_VII%2C_1801.jpg/440px-Teodoro_Matteini%2C_Ritratto_di_Pio_VII%2C_1801.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1202" data-file-height="1567" /></a></td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color:gold;"><a href="/wiki/Pope" title="Pope">Pope</a>; <a href="/wiki/Confessor" title="Confessor">Confessor</a></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Born</th><td class="infobox-data">Barnaba Niccolò Maria Luigi Chiaramonti<br />14 August 1742<br /><a href="/wiki/Cesena" title="Cesena">Cesena</a>, <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data">20 August 1823 (aged 81)<br /><a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Palace" title="Apostolic Palace">Apostolic Palace</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, Papal States</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Venerated&#160;in</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church">Roman Catholic Church</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Saint_symbolism" title="Saint symbolism">Attributes</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Papal_vestments" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal vestments">Papal attire</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Papal_tiara" title="Papal tiara">Papal tiara</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Religious_habit" title="Religious habit">Benedictine habit</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Savona-Noli" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Savona-Noli">Diocese of Savona-Noli</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Persecution_of_Christians" title="Persecution of Christians">Persecuted Christians</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Prisoner" title="Prisoner">Prisoners</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>An application to commence beatification proceedings were lodged to the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> on 10 July 2006 and received the approval of Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Camillo_Ruini" title="Camillo Ruini">Camillo Ruini</a> (the <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Vicar_of_Rome" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal Vicar of Rome">Vicar of Rome</a>) who transferred the request to the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a>. The Congregation - on 24 February 2007 - approved the opening of the cause responding to the call of the Ligurian bishops.
</p><p>On 15 August 2007, the Holy See contacted the diocese of Savona-Noli with the news that <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> had declared "<a href="/wiki/Nihil_obstat" title="Nihil obstat">nihil obstat</a>" (nothing stands against) the cause of beatification of the late pontiff, thus opening the diocesan process for this pope's beatification. He now has the title of <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The official text declaring the opening of the cause was: "<i>Summus Pontifex Benedictus XVI declarat, ex parte Sanctae Sedis, nihil obstare quominus in Causa Beatificationis et Canonizationis Servi Dei Pii Barnabae Gregorii VII Chiaramonti</i>". Work on the cause commenced the following month in gathering documentation on the late pope.
</p><p>He has since been elected as the patron of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Savona-Noli" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Savona-Noli">Diocese of Savona</a> and the patron of prisoners.<sup id="cite_ref-Pope_Pius_VII_returned_to_Savona_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pope_Pius_VII_returned_to_Savona-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In late 2018 the Bishop of Savona announced that the cause for Pius VII would continue following the completion of initial preparation and investigation. The bishop named a new postulator and a diocesan tribunal which would begin work into the cause.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The formal introduction to the cause (a diocesan investigation into the late pontiff's life) was held at a Mass celebrated in the Savona diocese on 31 October 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The first <a href="/wiki/Postulator" title="Postulator">postulator</a> for the cause was Father Giovanni Farris (2007–18) and the current postulator since 2018 is Fr. Giovanni Margara.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Monuments">Monuments</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Monuments">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Pope Pius VII's monument (1831) in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a>, adorning his tomb, was created by the <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Danish</a> sculptor <a href="/wiki/Bertel_Thorvaldsen" title="Bertel Thorvaldsen">Bertel Thorvaldsen</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Bibliography">Bibliography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Bibliography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Motu_proprio"><a href="/wiki/Motu_proprio" title="Motu proprio">Motu proprio</a></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Motu proprio">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Papal_bull"><a href="/wiki/Papal_bull" title="Papal bull">Papal bull</a></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_VII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Papal bull">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
